# 2026-07-29 — TASK-027 live validation: companion pointing works end to end
- Ran the prototype on the user's machine with real Screen Recording/microphone grants and a live GROQ_API_KEY. Found and fixed six defects: dotted Tauri event names (`annotate.marks` and `sensor.capture` were rejected at emit and had never delivered — the blink tell and every annotation were dead on arrival), reasoning-model `<think>` leakage plus empty answers presented as success, the missing `annotate*` entry in `capabilities/default.json` that silently denied the annotation webview the core `plugin:event|listen` permission, an SVG `calc()` frame and an `opacity: 0` entrance animation that stopped the surface painting, and an ordinary always-on-top annotate window that could never draw over a fullscreen app (now an all-Spaces NSPanel like the Avatar).
- Adopted the user-chosen two-stage grid locator: the model's raw coordinates are discarded (measured unreliable), stage 1 rides along with the answer as a `[CELL:..]` tag, stage 2 refines on a padded crop, and the mark is sized to the located region. Image downscaling to 1280px plus the folded stage cut a pointing ask from three provider calls to two, which brought it under the free tier's 8,000 tokens/minute and let refinement succeed.
- Verified live: summon by hover/menu/⌘⇧Space, consented capture, correct screen description, yellow spotlight+callout rendered over a fullscreen app, ~12s auto-clear, and push-to-talk Whisper transcription. Rust 72/72, web 106/106, typecheck and build pass. Honest remaining gap is pointing accuracy (weak model grounding); evidence and options in `outputs/2026-07-29-task-027-companion-clicky-parity.md`.

# 2026-07-29 — TASK-027 Set-of-Mark pointing + Privacy Guard; TASK-028 queued
- Fixed companion pointing accuracy without changing the vision model, by adopting clicky-windows' actual technique: the numbered locator grid is now DRAWN onto the screenshot (12x8 coarse on the answer image, 6x6 on the zoomed crop) instead of being described in words, so the model reads a printed cell number rather than estimating pixels. This is Set-of-Mark / Mark-Grid Scaffold prompting; grid rendering is self-contained via a hand-rolled 5x7 digit font. Sample overlay: `outputs/2026-07-29-task-027-set-of-mark-grid.png`.
- Adopted Privacy Guard from the same repo, applied at Bridge's egress boundary rather than at capture: a screenshot of a password manager, keychain, or authenticator window is refused for cloud egress regardless of the consent tick, with a typed `COMPANION_PRIVACY_GUARD` error. Rust 75/75.
- Surveyed all four clicky repos for further features. Confirmed none performs autonomous computer control, so the user's requested browser-research capability is Bridge's own design, not a port. Recorded R-042 and appended TASK-028 with plan `docs/raw/autonomous-browser-research-agent-plan-2026-07.md`: a bounded multi-step Research Run reusing the existing keyless SearchProvider, Agent/child-Run orchestration, and Proposal→Decision gating, with page text quarantined as hostile input. BR0 (step loop over search only, no new binary, no new authority) is the first slice; the managed browser binary and any click/type authority each need explicit approval first.
- Live re-test confirms the drawn grid works: coarse cell 60 of 96 and fine cell 15 of 36 located the target in a 76x101px region, putting the ring ~12 logical px from the real control versus ~440 with the imagined grid — a ~15x improvement from the same model and the same two provider calls. TASK-027 pointing accuracy is resolved; remaining V-items are the local-path, degradation, multi-monitor, and regression checks. Evidence: `outputs/2026-07-29-task-027-accurate-pointing-evidence.png`.

# 2026-07-29 — TASK-028 Research Run engine (BR0/BR1/BR3/BR4/BR5)
- Added `@bridge/research`: a bounded, port-driven multi-step Research Run engine. Search, page reading, element location, actuation, planner, and ledger are all injected, so the authority model, bounds, evidence ledger, and injection defense are unit-tested with no network, browser, or model. 31 tests pass.
- Authority is engine-decided from tool and target, never planner-decided: reading and searching are autonomous; click/type emit a Proposal and park the Run until a Decision (AP-088); credentials, payment, publishing, and any action on a login/checkout/security page are refused outright and never proposed. Every exit path records a StopReason.
- Injection defense is structural first — external text is quarantined at the boundary and reaches the planner only as labelled observations, never in an instruction channel; the pattern detector is a tripwire on top, and a poisoned page is reported rather than obeyed, its text never becoming usable evidence.
- BR1 reads pages over HTTP with an HTML-to-text extractor rather than bundling a browser engine (AP-089 records the deferral and its reversal condition). Fixed a real leak found by its own test: an unclosed `<script>` previously spilled its source into the extracted prose, which is exactly where injected instructions would hide.
- BR4 resume replays ledger evidence as context without re-executing it, and carries spent step/page/byte budget across the restart.
- Not yet built: BR2 element locating (needs a rendering surface — the webview-backed reader is the next slice) and the live wiring (API procedure, Run detail Page, companion entry point). The engine is complete and tested but not yet reachable from the UI.

# 2026-07-30 — TASK-028 webview reader + Set-of-Mark page locator (BR1 escalation, BR2)
- Added `research_webview.rs` to the desktop shell: the rendering surface AP-089 chose over a bundled Chromium. JS-rendered pages load in a real webview; an injected script waits for the page to settle, then ships its visible text out through a cancelled `bridge-extract:` navigation — an outbound-only channel, so the page gains no inbound path into the shell.
- Containment for a surface that renders adversarial content by design: the window label is absent from `capabilities/default.json` (zero Tauri permissions), the external origin never receives IPC injection at all, `on_navigation` refuses everything but http(s), and the window gets its own minimal init script — never the main window's, which carries the sidecar token. The reader window is VISIBLE, pinned always-on-bottom and never focused: the user can watch what Bridge reads at any time (no silent browsing — the blink-tell principle applied to a browser).
- BR2: `research_locate` captures the reader window's OWN image (`screencapture -l`, so overlapping windows can never leak into a locator frame) and reuses TASK-027's drawn-grid pipeline (coarse 12x8 → zoomed 6x6) to resolve a described element to a logical viewport rectangle; the window is undecorated so image and viewport coincide. Returns None rather than a guessed rectangle when the model cannot see the target.
- Commands `research_read_page`/`research_locate`/`research_close` registered; the extraction cap is enforced in the (hostile-territory, advisory) page script AND re-enforced at decode with char-boundary truncation. 5 new unit tests cover the navigation policy, payload round-trip, and truncation; desktop suite 80 passing.
- Still not built: the live wiring (API procedure, Run detail Page, "Research this" companion entry point). Still not live-validated: real-page extraction, `-l` capture of an always-on-bottom window across Spaces, and locate accuracy on a real page.

# 2026-07-30 — TASK-028 prototype wiring: "Research this" in the companion panel
- Per the user's explicit prototype-first decision, the companion ask panel gains a Research tab: `ResearchRun.tsx` runs the `@bridge/research` engine in the overlay webview with search wired to the governed `agentOrchestration.skill.webResearch` procedure (TASK-023 lane: Tier-1 keyless, quarantined, cited), read/find wired to the shell's `research_read_page`/`research_locate`, and the planner wired to the new `createChatPlanner` over a narrow text-only `research_chat` command — the Groq key never enters a webview. No actuator or proposal channel is wired, so the engine blocks every amber step honestly: green tools only.
- `createChatPlanner` lives in `@bridge/research` (transport-agnostic: the kernel can inject its ModelProvider later unchanged). Channel discipline holds in the planner too: observations enter the prompt only inside `fenceUntrusted` fences, and a test asserts the hostile text appears nowhere outside them. Strict JSON step parsing; garbage, unknown tools, and empty arguments throw into the engine's `planner_failed` path. 7 new tests, package at 38.
- Recorded deviation: Run steps execute in the overlay, not yet as kernel child Runs (plan §2's end state); kernel-Run migration and the Run detail Page are the follow-up. Verified: research 38/38, desktop Rust 80/80, web 106/106, typecheck + production build pass. Live end-to-end validation (real page, capture, locate, overlay auth for webResearch) remains open.

# 2026-07-30 — TASK-028 live validation: a Research Run works end to end
- First fully-live Run on the user's machine: 8 steps, governed search with Local Plane quarantine verdicts, real page reads in the contained always-on-bottom reader, a brief with per-claim citations, honest stop at bound_wall_clock, 22 citations.
- Six live defects found and fixed: the overlay window carried no sidecar capability so every governed mutation 401'd (SEC-1 fail-closed — overlay now carries the origin-gated token; annotate stays tokenless); the webResearch content guard captured its provider at sidecar boot and never saw the local model that starts ~30s later (now resolved lazily per inspection — recovery added, fail-closed unchanged); the 2.5GB Qwen install was stranded in `local-plane/model-runtime` while the supervisor watches the sibling `local-plane.model-runtime` (moved); dev builds had no llama-server (provisioned the pinned b9000 runtime via the repo's own prepareLlamaRuntime, hash-verified); typed shell/tRPC errors collapsed to "[object Object]" in the planner's own history, blinding it into retry loops (normalised everywhere); and two process aborts traced via crash reports to Tauri commands answering after WKWebView's ~60s IPC deadline stops the scheme task (all provider calls now hard-capped: local 20s, cloud 15s, companion 90s→40s; BUGS.md entry records the residual locate/ask stacking risk).
- The planner now runs LOCAL-FIRST (no rate limit, fenced page text never leaves the machine) with cloud fallback and a spaced 429 retry; the engine never re-reads a URL (test-pinned) and plans over only the freshest observations. Research 39/39, desktop Rust 80/80, api wiring 13/13, web 106/106.

# 2026-07-31 — TASK-028 kernel-Run migration: durable Research Runs, steps as child Runs, Run detail Page
- Closed the recorded prototype deviation (ADR-156): a Research Run is now a durable, owner-scoped kernel record. `agentOrchestration.research.start` mints the run + its Goal/Task + a parent-Run envelope id; every executed step lands through `recordStep` as a TERMINAL child Agent Run — ledger-audited, authority/taint-narrowed, inspectable through the same `childRun.*` surface every other delegation uses — plus an append-only evidence row. Migration `0035` adds `research_runs`/`research_run_steps` with chat-style owner FORCE RLS, append-only steps (no UPDATE/DELETE grant), and a trigger freezing terminal rows.
- BR4 resume is now real in the live path: the engine's ledger port reads/writes the kernel, so an interrupted Run (executor died mid-loop) is offered for resume in the panel and replays durable evidence without re-executing or re-spending budget. Quarantined page text persists for exactly that purpose and is withheld from every other read — the new `/research` Run detail Page renders engine-authored summaries with untrusted-external markers, never raw page text.
- Interruption is cross-surface and cooperative: the Page's Stop raises `stopRequested` in the kernel; the executor polls it into the engine's abort signal at the next step edge. A Run whose executor is gone can be closed honestly as cancelled — never given a fabricated outcome. Step child Runs record at "notify" on the local plane: green-tier steps already executed autonomously under AP-088, and a retroactive "approve" would fake a Human decision.
- Also fixed in passing: the `migration-metadata` journal pin was already broken on main (0034 landed without updating it) — rebased to 0035 with snapshot assertions for 0034/0035. Verified: core 485/485, db 217/217 (fresh migration, no-op generation, RLS two-member proof, terminal-trigger proof, restart durability), api 388 pass/1 pre-existing skip (4 new research lifecycle/privacy/validation tests), web 106/106 + typecheck + production build; changed-file lint clean. Live browser evidence of the new Page and a live resumed Run remain open (dev server owned by a concurrent session today).

# 2026-07-31 — TASK-028/TASK-027 start-then-poll commands close the WKWebView ~60s abort class
- BUG-2026-07-30's residual is resolved structurally: `companion_ask`, `research_locate`, and `research_chat` no longer answer inline. Each is now a `_start` command (validates, spawns the provider work detached, returns a job id immediately) plus an instant `_poll`, over a shared take-once job table (`jobs.rs`) — stale entries purged, growth capped at 32, ids never reused, and completion side effects (annotation marks, speech) run on the first poll that observes the finished job, exactly once. No command ever holds a WKWebView IPC reply open while a provider call runs, so the abort class is closed by construction; the old direct command forms are deleted rather than left as a live crash surface.
- Frontend drives the pairs through one `tauriInvokeJob` helper with client-side deadlines (ask/locate 120s, planner chat 90s at a 700ms poll). Verified: desktop Rust 84 pass + 1 ignored (4 new job-table tests; the one clippy doc warning is pre-existing in sensor_bridge), web 106/106 + typecheck + production build, changed-file lint clean. Live re-validation of an ask and a locate under the new shape rides on the next companion session.

# 2026-07-31 — Full-monorepo sweep (TASK-027 V11), dev model-start stall fixed, orphan llama-server reaped
- TASK-027's V11 regression sweep ran on the tree carrying every companion change plus the TASK-028 kernel-Run migration and the start-then-poll command shape: full `turbo run test` 42/42 tasks successful (7m48s). The remaining TASK-027 V-items (V2 local ask, V7/V8 degradation, V9 multi-monitor, V10 blink) all need the user at the keyboard and stay open.
- Live investigation of why the relaunched dev app had no local model: the supervisor thread was sitting in `launch → sha256_file`, re-verifying the pinned ~2.5GB model with sha2's software fallback in an unoptimized build — an hour-plus stall after every dev rebuild, during which the webResearch content guard, local-first planner, and local ask were all silently unavailable. Fixed with dev-profile `opt-level = 3` overrides for the four crypto crates (release unchanged; the fail-closed hash check itself is deliberately NOT cached or weakened). Also found and terminated a 2.5GB llama-server orphaned by one of 2026-07-30's abnormal app deaths (guard gap recorded as OPEN in BUGS.md), and V2 is now genuinely unblocked: managed Qwen installed, runtime resources present, local ask path code-complete.

# 2026-08-01 — WhatsApp Module (TASK-029): contained session, read-op allowlist, Local Plane roster
- New `whatsapp` built-in Module. Nav → the owner's OWN WhatsApp Web session in a contained Tauri webview (`whatsapp_webview.rs`), sibling Tools Page hosting the Contact Extractor. Engine is vendored, SHA-256-pinned `@wppconnect/wa-js` v4.5.0 (Apache-2.0) injected at document start; `build.rs` now fails the build on a hash mismatch. Containment follows the research reader and narrows it: navigation restricted to whatsapp.com/.net, because this webview holds a live authenticated session. The web app names an operation from a fixed allowlist and can never supply JavaScript — that, not convention, is what makes v1 read-only.
- Three facts came out of the live spike and are encoded in the code: WhatsApp Web serves an "update Safari" wall to WKWebView's default UA (needs an explicit `Version/` token); a linked session DOES survive a process restart despite `aquire-persistent-storage-denied`, correcting an earlier read of that error as fatal; and liveness is a CONNECTED socket plus a populated store, since `WPP.isReady` flips false once the socket connects and reading earlier fails with "sendIq called before startComms".
- Running against the real account (8,384 contacts · 817 groups · 35,298 unique participants) found two defects in already-committed code. WhatsApp Linked IDs were being turned into fabricated phone numbers for 4,203 of 8,384 contacts; the first fix was insufficient because the extraction layer had already laundered LID digits into a `phone` field, so `phoneFor` now short-circuits on the id itself and the two identity spaces stay disjoint. Scale also forced a participant policy — staging every group member would have meant 35,298 People against the 998 actually known.
- Residency corrected mid-session on the user's instruction: a staged cloud-canonical import was withdrawn, and contacts now land on the Local Plane. `local_people` gains local-only `phones` plus a source-scoped `dedupe_key` (with an explicit additive-column migration, since `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` would have left installed databases on the old shape), and new `local_person_lists` keep a bulk roster out of the relationship graph.
- v2 built in the same session: `decideSend` implements approve-per-recipient-then-trusted, with grants bound to the exact message body so standing trust is not a blank cheque, and revocation beating any live approval. Verified: desktop Rust 6/6 WhatsApp tests, module package 47/47, Google intake 5/5 (fixture widened for the new store methods), Local Plane list test, API + web typecheck clean. Task 13's live exit test through a real `tauri dev` build remains open.

# 2026-08-02 — WhatsApp becomes write-enabled with a local searchable store (TASK-030, ADR-158, AP-091)
- Design settled over several rounds and reversed two things TASK-029 recorded. The user made the architectural call themselves: our Chats surface is ALREADY a real WhatsApp Web client running WhatsApp's own code with wa-js injected — the same technique `whatsapp-web.js` uses, only visible — so adding it as a backend would mean a second authenticated client for one account. Rule adopted verbatim: one account, one browser profile, one wa-js runtime, many surfaces. I had argued for Baileys on memory grounds and was beaten by a better argument (it reimplements the protocol and is structurally distinguishable from a real client); dropping multi-account removed my objection entirely.
- **Residency reversed, explicitly.** ADR-157 kept message bodies out of Bridge. TASK-030 stores them on the Local Plane and indexes them. Verified rather than assumed that this needs no new infrastructure: pglite 0.2.17 ships `pg_trgm` and `vector`, and Postgres full-text search is core. Encryption at rest is recorded as an accepted, documented limitation — pglite writes to the user's home directory and FileVault is their setting, not our guarantee. Also set the expectation that a linked device never receives full history, so the index grows forward rather than retroactively.
- **Read-only reversed.** v1 now sends. `decideSend` (built during TASK-029) decides policy in TypeScript; the rate ceiling moves to Rust, because a renderer-side cap is bypassable and a cap that does not bind is not protection. The discipline is behavioural and deliberately binding — consent gate (never a first contact), hard daily cap, per-recipient cooldown, refusal of near-identical bodies, warm-up, recipient-timezone hours, kill switch that never auto-resumes. Jitter is explicitly not treated as a cloak.
- Two reuse intakes ran with licences verified from LICENSE files rather than badges. **I was wrong** to claim no adoptable WhatsApp-like React frontend exists: `matiasbattocchia/open-bsp-ui` is Unlicense, maintained, React 19 + Tailwind v4 and genuinely complete — but unvirtualized and hard-typed on Supabase BSP rows, so it is a pattern donor, not a dependency. The user then asked for a makeshift UI rather than a replica, which drops the pixel work and removes the trade-dress question. Legally unusable (no LICENSE at all): `jazimabbas/whatsapp-web-ui`, `whatsweb-chat`, both `wacraft` code repos. Legally blocked: `open-wa` is Hippocratic 1.1, not MIT as widely claimed, with its own package.json files contradicting it.
- `karem505/whatRust` (MIT, Tauri v2) adopted as shell reference; checking it against our own code found two live defects filed in BUGS.md — no `on_download` handler (so WhatsApp's download control fails silently) and no macOS `data_store_identifier`, the leading unproven candidate for the `aquire-persistent-storage-denied` noise. Deliberately NOT copying its `userAgentData` shim: that exists because it claims Chrome, and our Safari UA is self-consistent without it.
- TASK-030 opened as a NEW task rather than growth of TASK-029, on the user's challenge — it does not share TASK-029's exit test and exceeds the ~30% same-surface allowance several times over. TASK-029 stays open on its own unwalked exit test and owns the two defect fixes.

# 2026-08-02 (later) — TASK-030 tracks A/B/C land; the embed question is settled against my own recommendation
- Three parallel worktree agents, split by file ownership so they could not collide. All three found their worktree created from a stale base and reset themselves to the TASK-030 commit before starting — worth fixing in how these are launched, not in their work.
- **I was wrong about the embed, and Track A proved it.** I had verified that `Window::add_child` exists under `unstable` and that `WebviewBuilder` carries `user_agent`, `initialization_script` and `on_navigation`, and told the user "the entire security boundary transfers unchanged... that was the open risk, and it's closed." It was not closed. A capability declaring `windows: ["main"]` applies to EVERY webview in that window regardless of the `webviews` field, so as a child webview the session would LOSE the capability exclusion that keeps Tauri IPC off a page holding a live authenticated account. Separately, `get_webview_window("main")` returns `None` once main hosts a second webview — 22 call sites with silent `(0.0, 0.0)` fallbacks, the same silent-degradation shape as the off-screen-window defect this Module already hit. Spike reverted; the parented child window stands. The user's preference for a single entity does not survive the security boundary, and that is the honest answer rather than a workaround.
- **Two integration defects, neither caught by any test.** A raw NUL byte in `whatsapp_webview.rs` (an event-batcher composite key inside a Rust raw string) compiled cleanly and passed 102 tests while making the file binary to every text tool — `grep` suppresses all matches in a file containing NUL, so security-relevant greps against the file holding the read-op allowlist came back looking clean and empty. Found only because a grep for a string I knew was present returned nothing. And Tracks A and B independently chose different names for the same event channel (`whatsapp://session-events` vs `whatsapp:events`); nothing failed loudly because B degrades silently when the channel is absent. Reconciled to `whatsapp:session-events`, the repo convention — dotted names have never delivered here.
- **Track C corrected my storage premise.** pglite 0.2.17 ships `pg_trgm` but does not OFFER it: `CREATE EXTENSION` fails unless the module is registered at client construction. Sharper, and verified: a trigram index in a persisted database reopened without the extension breaks every query touching that table — including plain `ILIKE` and `count(*)` — and the catalog cannot detect it, because the `pg_extension` row survives so `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS` succeeds as a no-op. So core full-text is unconditional and trigram is conditional, probed functionally rather than from the catalog.
- Verified after integration: desktop Rust 102 pass (1 ignored), `@bridge/whatsapp` 87, `@bridge/local` 37, `@bridge/web` 115 — including a test asserting the TypeScript and Rust op allowlists match so the two adapter layers cannot drift apart.
- Honest gaps carried forward: the Rust-enforced send ceiling does NOT exist yet (today's cap is renderer-side and bypassable, which is exactly what ADR-158 says is not protection); no retention or deletion path for the message store, so it grows unbounded; encryption at rest still unsolved; and nothing here has been run against a live session.

# 2026-08-02 (later still) — the send ceiling binds in Rust; the embed question is reopened toward a hidden engine
- The Rust-enforced ceiling landed, closing the gap I had flagged as the honest hole: until now the daily cap lived only in the renderer, which ADR-158 itself calls "not protection". Cap, per-recipient cooldown and a sticky kill switch now bind in `whatsapp_send.rs` against a durable ledger, and the restart property is proven by a test that spends the allowance, discards every in-memory structure, and re-reads from disk sharing nothing but a file path. Four sub-decisions recorded in the ADR addendum; the one that matters most is that a corrupt ledger HALTS rather than reading as empty, because otherwise corrupting it would be a way to reset the cap.
- First write op shipped, and the obsolete "no write op exists" assertion was rewritten rather than deleted — the read path still refuses `send_message`, `eval`, `Function` and everything unlisted, and the send script is reachable only from the gated command. Body escaping targets the JavaScript lexer including U+2028/U+2029, the classic gap in a naive escaper.
- Verified after merge: Rust 120 pass (was 102), `@bridge/whatsapp` 95, `@bridge/local` 37, `@bridge/web` 120.
- **The user rejected the parent/child window for the third time**, and they were right to keep pushing. I had been defending the architecture instead of solving the problem — first a child window, then arguing the `unstable` child webview was impossible on security grounds (true), and stopping there. The option I should have driven to two rounds earlier: make the WhatsApp window INVISIBLE and render chats in Bridge's own DOM from the Local Plane store. That is genuinely one integrated app, and it KEEPS the capability exclusion rather than trading it away, because a hidden window still has its own label. Strictly better than both options I had been weighing against each other.
- Spike now running on the one real unknown: whether macOS throttles an unmapped WKWebView badly enough to break WhatsApp's background sync, with far-offscreen positioning as the fallback. The consequence, flagged to the user before it is expensive to reverse: hiding WhatsApp means Bridge owns the chat surface permanently — replies, reactions, voice notes, group admin — and gets whatever WhatsApp ships next only if we build it.
